        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Port useful old VibeComfy capabilities into the new package

## Overview
The new `vibecomfy` package is centered on `VibeWorkflow` as the only editable IR, with JSON as input/output and embedded Comfy as the default runtime. The brief calls for two parallel tracks:

1. **Structural cleanup** of the package surface so port work has a clean home: split the monolithic `vibecomfy/cli.py`, dedupe the three RunPod runner scripts, fix wrong `sources sync` defaults that point at `vendor/workflow_templates` (which doesn't exist locally) instead of `workflow_corpus/`, kill dead/empty paths (top-level `input/`, `docs/comfyscript_spike/`), rename or split `vibecomfy/templates/` (it currently does both ingest and registry), and add module homes `vibecomfy/schema/`, `vibecomfy/analysis/`, `vibecomfy/search/`.
2. **Port-early features** from the old `peteromallet/VibeComfy`: schema-aware validation backed by `/object_info`, a reusable graph-analysis module with CLI surface (`analyze`, `trace`, `upstream`, `downstream`, `path`, `subgraph`, `values`, `diff`, `unconnected`), and a node-search module with weighted scoring + task aliases (`i2v`, `controlnet`, `wan`, `ltx`, `audio reactive`) that plugs into `/object_info`, `node_index.json`, custom-node examples, and curated metadata.

Port-after-redesign work (editing primitives, SVG/visualization, MCP adapter) is **out of scope for this plan** per the brief's staging — left for a follow-up plan once these APIs settle.

Repository touch points called out in the idea, with current evidence:
- `vibecomfy/cli.py:182-186` — `sources sync` defaults `vendor/workflow_templates` / `vendor/external_workflows` / `custom_nodes`. Only `vendor/direct_templates/` exists; the real corpus is `workflow_corpus/{official,custom_nodes,manifests}`.
- `vibecomfy/cli.py` is one ~267-line file with all subcommands; needs to become `vibecomfy/commands/{sources,workflows,nodes,inspect,convert,validate,doctor,run,runtime,logs}.py` with a thin `cli.py` aggregator.
- `vibecomfy/templates/{loader,normalize,index,library}.py` — `loader/normalize/index` ingest JSON; `library` is a registry. Brief asks to rename to `ingest/` (or split `templates/` + `normalize/`).
- `scripts/runpod_validate.py`, `scripts/runpod_model_matrix.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py` — share `PodGuard`, `_should_skip`, `_upload_dir`, signal install, terminate-on-exit. Each is 145–356 lines; common extractable surface ≈ 100 lines.
- `input/` (top-level) is empty / unused — `.gitignore` has rules, but no in-repo code references `input/3d`.
- `docs/comfyscript_spike/` is an empty directory; `docs/comfyscript_spike.md` exists alongside as the actual content.
- `vibecomfy/workflow.py:120-129` — `validate()` only checks emptiness and dangling edges; doesn't see `/object_info`.
- `vibecomfy/runtime/` already has `client.py`/`server.py`/`run.py` — schema fetching can hit the same managed/embedded runtime.
- `runpod_*` script entrypoints must stay callable as `python scripts/runpod_validate.py` etc. (the runpod scripts hardcode `vendor/workflow_templates` paths in `EXCLUDE_DIRS` and candidate lists — those need updating but the entrypoint contract is preserved).

Constraints honored throughout:
- VibeWorkflow stays the only editable IR; analysis is read-only on it.
- JSON is input/output only — no new "edit JSON" CLI surface.
- Embedded runtime stays the default for `vibecomfy run`.
- All existing CLI subcommands keep their names, args, and exit codes.
- The three RunPod entrypoint script names stay the same.

## Phase 1: Structural cleanup (mechanical, no behavior change)

### Step 1: Audit dead/empty paths and document the rename targets
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** `input/` top-level is genuinely unused: `Grep` for `^input/` or string `"input/3d"` across the package — already confirmed only `.gitignore` and the runpod scripts reference an `input` directory, and the runpod scripts create their own at runtime (`mkdir -p input` in remote scripts). Local `input/3d/` is empty.
2. **Verify** `docs/comfyscript_spike/` is empty and content is fully captured by `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`. Already confirmed: directory has no children.
3. **Decide** rename direction for `vibecomfy/templates/`: choose **split** into `vibecomfy/ingest/` (loader + normalize + index) and `vibecomfy/registry/` (library = the template-index lookup). See assumptions.
4. **No code yet** — produce a checklist of move targets to execute in Step 2-3.

### Step 2: Delete dead paths and fix `sources sync` defaults (`vibecomfy/cli.py`, `input/`, `docs/comfyscript_spike/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Delete** `input/` top-level directory (and its sole empty child `3d/`).
2. **Delete** empty directory `docs/comfyscript_spike/` (keep the `.md` file alongside).
3. **Fix** `vibecomfy/cli.py:183-185` defaults so `vibecomfy sources sync` (no args) works against the real local corpus:
   - `--official` default → `workflow_corpus/official`
   - `--external` default → `workflow_corpus/custom_nodes`
   - `--custom-nodes` default stays `custom_nodes` (still where ComfyUI custom node packs land at runtime)
4. **Update** `runpod_validate.py:20` `EXCLUDE_DIRS` entry `"vendor/workflow_templates"` → `"vendor/direct_templates"` (or drop, since vendored corpus is small) so uploads stay lean.
5. **Update** `runpod_model_matrix.py:22-32` candidate paths that point at `vendor/workflow_templates/templates/...` to use `workflow_corpus/official/...` paths now present in the manifest. Keep external candidates as-is (they live under `examples/` and `custom_nodes/`).
6. **Decide** fate of `vendor/direct_templates/` (24 vendored Comfy-Org template JSONs): leave in place and document it as a frozen offline copy used by examples/runpod scripts, OR delete it because `workflow_corpus/official/` is now canonical. See questions; default assumption is **leave in place** as offline reference, but stop pointing CLI defaults at it.

### Step 3: Split monolithic `cli.py` into `vibecomfy/commands/` (`vibecomfy/cli.py`, new `vibecomfy/commands/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py` exposing each command module's `register(subparsers)` function.
2. **Move** each `_cmd_*` handler + its `add_parser` block from `vibecomfy/cli.py` into a per-command module:
   - `commands/sources.py` (sync)
   - `commands/workflows.py` (list)
   - `commands/nodes.py` (list)
   - `commands/inspect.py`
   - `commands/convert.py`
   - `commands/validate.py`
   - `commands/doctor.py`
   - `commands/run.py`
   - `commands/runtime.py` (doctor, smoke)
   - `commands/logs.py`
3. **Reduce** `vibecomfy/cli.py` to: `build_parser()` that walks the registered command modules, `main()`, and the shared `_resolve_workflow_path()` helper (which can move to `vibecomfy/registry/resolve.py` if both `inspect` and `convert` need it; assumption: keep in `cli.py` for now since both call sites are CLI).
4. **Confirm** `vibecomfy = "vibecomfy.cli:main"` script entry in `pyproject.toml:24` still works without changes.
5. **Run** `python -m pytest -q tests` to confirm no behavior drift.

### Step 4: Rename `vibecomfy/templates/` → split `vibecomfy/ingest/` + `vibecomfy/registry/` (`vibecomfy/templates/`, `vibecomfy/__init__.py`, all imports)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Move**:
   - `vibecomfy/templates/loader.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/loader.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/normalize.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/index.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/index.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/library.py` → `vibecomfy/registry/library.py`
2. **Update** all imports in `vibecomfy/cli.py` (now `commands/*.py`), `vibecomfy/__init__.py`, `vibecomfy/scratchpad_loader.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py`, and the three runpod scripts.
3. **Add** `vibecomfy/ingest/__init__.py` and `vibecomfy/registry/__init__.py` re-exporting the previously-public symbols (`load_template`, `detect_workflow_shape`, `normalize_to_api`, `convert_to_vibe_format`, `index_workflows`, `write_index`, `workflow_from_file`, `workflow_from_template`).
4. **Remove** the old `vibecomfy/templates/` directory completely (no shim — clean break per CLAUDE.md feedback).
5. **Run** tests + `python -m vibecomfy.cli sources sync` against `workflow_corpus/` to prove the wiring.

### Step 5: Dedupe RunPod scripts behind `scripts/runpod_runner.py` (`scripts/runpod_runner.py`, `scripts/runpod_validate.py`, `scripts/runpod_model_matrix.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `scripts/runpod_runner.py` exposing:
   - `class PodGuard` (current shared shape — `launch()` / `terminate()` / `_start_watchdog()` / `_terminate_after()`), parameterized on `name_prefix` and `max_runtime_seconds_env`.
   - `should_skip(path, root, exclude)` and `upload_dir(sftp, local, remote, exclude)` helpers.
   - `install_signal_handlers(loop)`.
   - `async def run_pod(remote_script: str, *, name_prefix: str, exclude: set[str], upload_mode: Literal["sftp_walk", "tarball"] = "sftp_walk", timeout: int) -> int` orchestrating launch → wait → upload → exec → terminate.
2. **Rewrite** `runpod_validate.py` to import from `runpod_runner` and define only its `EXCLUDE_DIRS`, the remote bash script string, and `main()`. Keep file path + entrypoint identical.
3. **Same** for `runpod_model_matrix.py` and `runpod_corpus_matrix.py` — the latter keeps its tarball upload path because of size; the helper supports both modes.
4. **Verify** all three scripts still parse and `python -c "import scripts.runpod_validate"` (or equivalent file run with no GPU) doesn't error before the network call.
5. **Defer** actually launching pods to the user — these are billable; do not auto-run.

### Step 6: Expand `README.md`
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** the 12-line stub with a README that covers: project goal, the new module layout (`vibecomfy/{workflow,ingest,registry,runtime,schema,analysis,search,commands}`), CLI subcommand list with one-line descriptions, the `sources sync` → `workflows list` → `inspect` → `convert` → `validate` → `run` quick start against `workflow_corpus/`, and links to `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md` and `docs/vibeworkflow.md`.
2. **Keep** the existing single-line flow diagram and `out/` mention.

## Phase 2: Schema-aware validation (`vibecomfy/schema/`)

### Step 7: Add a schema provider that talks to `/object_info` (`vibecomfy/schema/__init__.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/cache.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** `NodeSchema` dataclass — `class_type: str`, `pack: str | None`, `inputs: dict[str, InputSpec]` (where `InputSpec = {type: str, required: bool, default, choices, min, max}`), `outputs: list[OutputSpec]` (`type, name`).
2. **Implement** `RuntimeSchemaProvider`:
   - Calls the same managed/embedded runtime that `vibecomfy/runtime/server.py` already starts; reuses `ComfyClient` with a `GET /object_info` (or whichever endpoint `comfyui` exposes — verify in `vibecomfy/runtime/client.py` first; if missing, add a thin `httpx` GET).
   - Caches per-runtime fingerprint under `out/cache/object_info.<sha>.json`.
3. **Implement** `LocalSchemaProvider` — falls back to `node_index.json` (already produced by `sources sync`) when no runtime is reachable, providing `class_type`, `pack`, and a coarse input list.
4. **Expose** `get_schema_provider(prefer="runtime"|"local"|"auto")` factory that the rest of the package uses.

### Step 8: Use schema during UI→API normalization fallback (`vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **Today**, the fallback path at `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py:60-63` writes `widget_<i>` keys when `comfy.component_model.workflow_convert` isn't importable. With schema available, **map** widget positional values to real input names from `NodeSchema.inputs` (in declared order), preserving fallback to `widget_<i>` only when schema is unavailable for that `class_type`.
2. **Skip** any inputs already populated by linked edges (don't overwrite a connection with a positional widget value).

### Step 9: Wire schema-backed validation into `VibeWorkflow.validate()` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Keep** the existing structural checks in `VibeWorkflow.validate()` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:120`).
2. **Add** `vibecomfy/schema/validate.py:validate_against_schema(workflow, provider) -> list[ValidationIssue]` that checks:
   - `class_type` exists in schema (issue code `unknown_class_type`).
   - All `NodeSchema.inputs` marked required are present in `node.inputs ∪ node.widgets ∪ <incoming edges>` (`missing_required_input`).
   - No unknown keys in `node.inputs ∪ node.widgets` (`unknown_input`).
   - For each edge `from_node.from_output → to_node.to_input`, the source's declared output type matches the target's declared input type — string-equal or one is `*` / `ANY` (`type_mismatch`, severity `warning` initially since custom nodes lie about types).
3. **Surface** these via a new `VibeWorkflow.validate(schema_provider=None)` keyword: when `schema_provider` is not None, run both checks; otherwise keep current behavior so existing tests still pass.
4. **Update** `commands/validate.py` and `commands/doctor.py` to construct a provider via `get_schema_provider("auto")` and pass it in. Keep their stdout/exit-code contract.

### Step 10: Tests for schema validation (`tests/test_schema.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Cover** with a fake `SchemaProvider` (no real runtime):
   - Unknown class_type emits one issue.
   - Required input missing emits one issue with the input name in the message.
   - Unknown input emits one issue.
   - Type-mismatched edge emits one warning.
   - When `schema_provider=None`, validate() return value matches today's structural-only behavior (regression guard for `tests/test_workflow_core.py`).
2. **Run** `pytest -q tests/test_schema.py` then full suite.

## Phase 3: Graph analysis module (`vibecomfy/analysis/`)

### Step 11: Build the analysis primitives on `VibeWorkflow` (`vibecomfy/analysis/graph.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** as pure functions taking `VibeWorkflow`:
   - `analyze(workflow) -> dict` — node count, edge count, fan-in/fan-out histogram, output sinks, terminal inputs, detected media type.
   - `trace(workflow, node_id) -> list[VibeNode]` — full upstream chain leading to `node_id`, BFS by edges where `edge.to_node == cursor`.
   - `upstream(workflow, node_id, depth=None) -> set[str]` — ancestors.
   - `downstream(workflow, node_id, depth=None) -> set[str]` — descendants.
   - `path(workflow, src, dst) -> list[list[str]]` — all simple paths, capped at a sensible depth (e.g. 32).
   - `subgraph(workflow, node_ids) -> VibeWorkflow` — returns a new VibeWorkflow with only the given nodes and edges between them; **read-only**, never mutates the source.
   - `values(workflow, node_id=None) -> dict` — for a node (or all), the literal `inputs` + `widgets` flattened (skip linked inputs).
   - `diff(workflow_a, workflow_b) -> dict` — added/removed/changed nodes by id and class_type, edge add/remove sets, input value changes.
   - `unconnected(workflow) -> list[dict]` — nodes whose required inputs are not satisfied by edges or values (uses schema if provided; else just reports nodes with no incoming edges and no widget defaults for non-source nodes).
2. **Keep** all functions sync, no I/O — they operate purely on the in-memory IR.
3. **Reuse** the existing `VibeWorkflow` types; do not re-shape them.

### Step 12: Expose analysis as CLI subcommands (`vibecomfy/commands/analyze.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **Add** a single `vibecomfy analyze` parent subcommand with sub-subcommands `info` (= old `analyze`), `trace`, `upstream`, `downstream`, `path`, `subgraph`, `values`, `diff`, `unconnected`. (Brief lists nine analysis verbs; bundling under `analyze` avoids polluting the top-level subcommand list, but a flat layout is also acceptable — see questions.)
2. **Each** subcommand accepts a workflow positional (path or template id, resolved via the same resolver `inspect`/`convert` use), plus verb-specific args (`--node-id`, `--src`, `--dst`, `--max-depth`, `--format json|tsv|text`).
3. **Default** output is human-readable; `--format json` emits machine-readable.
4. **Wire** into `cli.py`'s parser registration.

### Step 13: Tests for analysis (`tests/test_analysis.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Build** a small synthetic VibeWorkflow (the one in `test_workflow_core.py:test_api_workflow_converts_to_vibe_workflow` is a good seed) and assert each function returns the expected sets/lists. Cover `diff` with a small mutation.
2. **CLI smoke**: `subprocess.run([..., 'vibecomfy', 'analyze', 'info', '<fixture>.json'])` returns 0 and prints non-empty stdout.

## Phase 4: Node search (`vibecomfy/search/`)

### Step 14: Build the search index and weighted scorer (`vibecomfy/search/index.py`, `vibecomfy/search/aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/search/scorer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** `SearchEntry` — `class_type`, `pack`, `description`, `tags: list[str]`, `tasks: list[str]`, `source: Literal["object_info","node_index","custom_node_examples","curated"]`, `path: str | None`.
2. **Build** the corpus by merging:
   - The runtime schema (Step 7) — gives full input/output type info per `class_type`.
   - `node_index.json` produced by `sources sync` — already covers runtime nodes.
   - Custom-node example workflows under `custom_nodes/*/example_workflows/` (via existing `vibecomfy/nodes/index.py:index_custom_node_examples`) — each example contributes the union of `class_type`s it uses, weighted lower.
   - Curated metadata from the official manifest (`workflow_corpus/manifests/coverage.json`) — gives `task` + `model_family` + `media`.
3. **Add** `vibecomfy/search/aliases.py` with the task-alias map: `i2v`, `t2v`, `t2i`, `controlnet`, `wan`, `ltx`, `audio reactive`, `flux`, `sdxl`, `qwen`, `hunyuan`, `inpaint`, `outpaint`, etc., each expanding to a list of substrings/regexes the scorer matches against `class_type`/`tags`/`description`.
4. **Implement** weighted scorer: exact `class_type` match (weight 5) > pack name match (weight 3) > tag match (weight 2) > description token (weight 1) > alias hit (weight 2 stacks). Return top-N sorted descending.

### Step 15: Add CLI commands `vibecomfy search` and `vibecomfy nodes spec` (`vibecomfy/commands/search.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/nodes.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **`vibecomfy search <query>`** — query is free text; supports `--task <alias>` and `--limit`. Prints id / class_type / pack / score / source.
2. **Extend** `vibecomfy nodes` (currently only `list`) with `nodes spec <class_type>` — print full `NodeSchema` for a single node from the schema provider. (Keep existing `nodes list`.)
3. **Wire** into `cli.py`.

### Step 16: Tests for search (`tests/test_search.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Fixture** with a small in-memory `SearchEntry` list; assert task aliases (`i2v` → expected hits), exact class_type wins over partial, pack-name match ranks above description-only match.
2. **Smoke** that the CLI command returns non-empty for `vibecomfy search wan` against the real corpus on a developer box.

## Phase 5: Validation rollup

### Step 17: Run the existing test suite + manual CLI smokes
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `python -m pytest -q tests` — all existing tests must still pass after the rename in Step 4 and the optional `schema_provider` arg in Step 9.
2. **Run** the CLI quick-start against the local corpus to prove sources/workflows/inspect/convert/validate/doctor still work end-to-end:
   ```bash
   python -m vibecomfy.cli sources sync
   python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows list --limit 5
   python -m vibecomfy.cli inspect z_image
   python -m vibecomfy.cli analyze info workflow_corpus/official/image/z_image.json
   python -m vibecomfy.cli search wan --task i2v
   ```
3. **Skip** GPU-bound runpod scripts in CI; just confirm `python -c "import scripts.runpod_validate"` succeeds (and same for the other two).

### Step 18: Confirm RunPod entrypoints still launch (info-only)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Document** in the PR description that `runpod_validate.py` / `runpod_model_matrix.py` / `runpod_corpus_matrix.py` were not actually run because they cost money; reviewer or user re-runs at their discretion. The dedupe in Step 5 keeps each entrypoint behavior-equivalent — `info` priority criterion only.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 first — structural cleanup is mechanical and unblocks the new module homes; doing it after the ports would force two rounds of import churn.
2. Within Phase 1: Steps 1→2→3→4→5→6 in order. Step 4 (rename) must happen before any new module imports it.
3. Phase 2 (schema) before Phase 3 and Phase 4 — both depend on the schema provider.
4. Phase 3 (analysis) and Phase 4 (search) can land in parallel after Phase 2 if reviewed separately, but as one plan they should serialize: 3 → 4 to keep diffs small.
5. Phase 5 last — validates the whole thing.

## Validation Order
1. After Phase 1, run `pytest -q tests` + a couple of CLI smokes to confirm no regression from the splits/renames.
2. After each of Steps 9, 11, 14: run the new module's targeted tests first, then full `pytest`.
3. After Phase 5, the manual CLI quick-start.
4. RunPod scripts: import-only check; do not auto-run pods.

  "assumptions": [
    "Port-after-redesign work (editing primitives copy/wire/set/delete/create/inline/batch, SVG/visualization, MCP adapter) is OUT of scope for this plan; this plan only ships structural cleanup + port-early features (schema validation, analysis, search). A follow-up plan handles port-after-redesign once these APIs are stable.",
    "The templates/ split is into vibecomfy/ingest/ (loader+normalize+index) and vibecomfy/registry/ (library), since loader/normalize/index do ingest work while library is a registry lookup. A flat rename to vibecomfy/ingest/ (with library.py inside it) is also acceptable if the user prefers \u2014 the brief allows either.",
    "vendor/direct_templates/ stays in the repo as a frozen offline mirror but no code path defaults to it after this PR. The CLI defaults move to workflow_corpus/{official,custom_nodes}/.",
    "Top-level input/ and docs/comfyscript_spike/ are confirmed dead (no in-repo references; .gitignore has rules for runtime-created input/ directories that the runpod scripts mkdir at runtime, so deletion of the empty local input/ is safe).",
    "vibecomfy/cli.py:_resolve_workflow_path stays in cli.py rather than moving to vibecomfy/registry/ \u2014 only CLI commands need it.",
    "Existing CLI subcommand contracts (subcommand names, flags, exit codes, stdout format) are preserved verbatim; new functionality lands as new subcommands or new optional flags, never breaking changes.",
    "vibecomfy.runtime stays where it is; the schema provider sits next to it and reuses ComfyClient/comfy_server primitives.",
    "RuntimeSchemaProvider can start the embedded runtime on demand (matching how `vibecomfy run --runtime embedded` works today) and caches /object_info results to disk; if a runtime can't be reached, LocalSchemaProvider serves stale node_index.json data.",
    "The analysis CLI surface is bundled under `vibecomfy analyze {verb}` rather than nine top-level verbs, keeping the top-level subcommand list small. Flat verbs are acceptable if user prefers.",
    "RunPod scripts are import-tested in CI but not actually launched (cost). The user re-runs them manually after this PR lands.",
    "No deprecation shim for the old vibecomfy.templates.* import path; clean break per project's no-shims preference. Imports across the package + tests + runpod scripts get updated in lockstep.",
    "VibeWorkflow.validate() keeps its current single-arg signature working (schema_provider defaults to None) so existing tests pass without edits."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []
}

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Port useful old VibeComfy capabilities into the new package

## Overview
The new `vibecomfy` package is centered on `VibeWorkflow` as the only editable IR; JSON is input/output only and embedded Comfy is the default runtime. This plan ships two tracks together:

1. **Structural cleanup** so the port has a clean home: split monolithic `vibecomfy/cli.py` into `vibecomfy/commands/`, dedupe the three RunPod scripts behind `scripts/runpod_runner.py`, fix wrong `sources sync` defaults that point at non-existent `vendor/workflow_templates`, kill dead/empty paths (`input/`, `docs/comfyscript_spike/`), split `vibecomfy/templates/` into `vibecomfy/ingest/` (loader + normalize + index) and `vibecomfy/registry/` (library), fix the latent template-id resolver bug (`workflow_from_template` ignores the external index whenever the official one exists), and add module homes `vibecomfy/schema/`, `vibecomfy/analysis/`, `vibecomfy/search/`.
2. **Port-early features**: schema-aware validation backed by `/object_info` (reusing the existing `ValidationIssue`/`ValidationReport` types), graph analysis (`analyze`/`trace`/`upstream`/`downstream`/`path`/`subgraph`/`values`/`diff`/`unconnected`) on `VibeWorkflow`, and node search with weighted scoring + task aliases (`i2v`/`t2v`/`t2i`/`controlnet`/`wan`/`ltx`/`audio_reactive`/`flux`/`sdxl`/`qwen`/`hunyuan`/`inpaint`/`outpaint`).

Port-after-redesign work (editing primitives, SVG/visualization, MCP adapter) is **out of scope** per the brief's staging — those land in a follow-up plan once these APIs settle.

Settled decisions carried forward as assumptions (no longer open questions): scope deferral, templates-split shape, clean break (no shims), keep `vendor/direct_templates/`, bundled `vibecomfy analyze {verb}` shape, preserved RunPod entrypoints, preserved CLI contracts.

Repository touch points (with current evidence):
- `vibecomfy/cli.py:182-186` — `sources sync` defaults reference `vendor/workflow_templates` / `vendor/external_workflows` (non-existent); real corpus is `workflow_corpus/{official,custom_nodes,manifests}`.
- `vibecomfy/cli.py` (~267 lines) holds every `_cmd_*` handler.
- `vibecomfy/templates/{loader,normalize,index,library}.py` — `loader/normalize/index` ingest JSON; `library` is registry lookup.
- `vibecomfy/templates/library.py:17-21` — current resolver: if `template_index.json` exists, **never** consults `external_workflow_index.json`; latent bug becomes painful for new analysis/search CLI accepting path-or-template-id across the corpus.
- `vibecomfy/workflow.py:120-129` — `validate()` only checks structural issues; returns `ValidationReport` with `ValidationIssue` objects (must be reused, not re-defined).
- `vibecomfy/workflow.py:120` callers: `vibecomfy/cli.py` (validate, doctor, run), `vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:28`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py:22`, `tests/test_scratchpad_loader.py`.
- `scripts/runpod_validate.py`, `runpod_model_matrix.py`, `runpod_corpus_matrix.py` — share `PodGuard`, `_should_skip`, `_upload_dir`, signal install. `runpod_corpus_matrix.py:155,168,181` have **embedded Python heredocs** that import `from vibecomfy.templates.loader import load_template` and `from vibecomfy.templates.normalize import normalize_to_api` — these strings must be updated lockstep with the rename.
- `tests/test_workflow_core.py:6-7` imports from `vibecomfy.templates`.
- `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:76` mentions templates conceptually, not the import path — no change needed.
- `input/` (top-level) is empty/unused; `docs/comfyscript_spike/` is empty (content lives in adjacent `.md`).

Constraints honored throughout:
- VibeWorkflow stays the only editable IR; analysis is read-only on it.
- JSON is input/output only — no new "edit JSON" CLI surface.
- Embedded runtime stays the default for `vibecomfy run`.
- All existing CLI subcommands keep their names, args, exit codes, and stdout shape.
- The three RunPod entrypoint script names stay the same.

## Phase 1: Structural cleanup (mechanical, no behavior change)

### Step 1: Delete dead paths and fix `sources sync` defaults (`vibecomfy/cli.py`, `input/`, `docs/comfyscript_spike/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Delete** top-level `input/` directory and its empty `3d/` child.
2. **Delete** empty `docs/comfyscript_spike/` directory (keep adjacent `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`).
3. **Fix** `vibecomfy/cli.py:183-185` defaults so `vibecomfy sources sync` (no flags) works against the local corpus:
   - `--official` default → `workflow_corpus/official`
   - `--external` default → `workflow_corpus/custom_nodes`
   - `--custom-nodes` default stays `custom_nodes` (where ComfyUI custom node packs land at runtime).
4. **Update** `runpod_validate.py:20` `EXCLUDE_DIRS` entry `"vendor/workflow_templates"` → `"vendor/direct_templates"` (offline mirror still exists; just exclude it from upload).
5. **Update** `runpod_model_matrix.py:22-32` `OFFICIAL_CANDIDATES` paths from `vendor/workflow_templates/templates/...` to the equivalents under `workflow_corpus/official/...` (keep external candidates as-is; they live under `examples/` and `custom_nodes/`).

### Step 2: Split monolithic `cli.py` into `vibecomfy/commands/` (`vibecomfy/cli.py`, new `vibecomfy/commands/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py` exposing each command module's `register(subparsers)`.
2. **Move** every `_cmd_*` handler + its `add_parser` block out of `vibecomfy/cli.py`:
   - `commands/sources.py` (sync), `commands/workflows.py` (list), `commands/nodes.py` (list), `commands/inspect.py`, `commands/convert.py`, `commands/validate.py`, `commands/doctor.py`, `commands/run.py`, `commands/runtime.py` (doctor + smoke), `commands/logs.py`.
3. **Reduce** `vibecomfy/cli.py` to: parser builder that walks registered command modules, `main()`, and the existing `_resolve_workflow_path()` helper (CLI-only consumers — keep here, don't move to registry/).
4. **Confirm** `pyproject.toml:24` script entry `vibecomfy = "vibecomfy.cli:main"` still works.
5. **Run** `python -m pytest -q tests` to confirm no behavior drift.

### Step 3: Split `vibecomfy/templates/` into `vibecomfy/ingest/` + `vibecomfy/registry/` (lockstep across all callers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Move** files:
   - `vibecomfy/templates/loader.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/loader.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/normalize.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/index.py` → `vibecomfy/ingest/index.py`
   - `vibecomfy/templates/library.py` → `vibecomfy/registry/library.py`
2. **Add** `vibecomfy/ingest/__init__.py` and `vibecomfy/registry/__init__.py` re-exporting public symbols (`load_template`, `detect_workflow_shape`, `normalize_to_api`, `convert_to_vibe_format`, `index_workflows`, `write_index`, `workflow_from_file`, `workflow_from_template`).
3. **Update every caller in lockstep** (no shim, clean break — full call set):
   - `vibecomfy/__init__.py` re-exports.
   - `vibecomfy/scratchpad_loader.py` (only references via `vibecomfy.workflow_from_*` re-exports — verify still works).
   - `vibecomfy/commands/*.py` (post-Step 2 locations of the old `_cmd_*` handlers).
   - `tests/test_workflow_core.py:6-7`.
   - **`scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`** — update three embedded Python heredocs at lines 155-156, 168-169, 181-182: `from vibecomfy.templates.loader` → `from vibecomfy.ingest.loader`; `from vibecomfy.templates.normalize` → `from vibecomfy.ingest.normalize`.
   - `scripts/runpod_validate.py` and `scripts/runpod_model_matrix.py` (no `vibecomfy.templates` imports today, but verify with `Grep` after the move).
4. **Remove** the old `vibecomfy/templates/` directory entirely.
5. **Verify** with `Grep -r "vibecomfy\.templates"` returning zero hits.
6. **Run** `pytest -q tests` and `python -m vibecomfy.cli sources sync` against `workflow_corpus/`.

### Step 4: Fix `workflow_from_template` to merge both index files (`vibecomfy/registry/library.py`, `vibecomfy/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Today**'s bug at `vibecomfy/templates/library.py:17-21` (post-rename `vibecomfy/registry/library.py`): if `template_index.json` exists, the resolver **never** consults `external_workflow_index.json`. `sources sync` writes both. Same shape exists in `vibecomfy/cli.py:_resolve_workflow_path` — it iterates both indexes, which is the correct behavior.
2. **Change** `workflow_from_template(template_id)` to **read both** `template_index.json` and `external_workflow_index.json` (whichever exist), concatenate entries, then look up `template_id`. Match by `id` first, then by `Path(path).stem` (today's behavior). Raise `KeyError` only when neither index has it.
3. **Add a regression test** in `tests/test_workflow_core.py` (or a new `tests/test_registry.py`): write both index files into `tmp_path`, register an id only in `external_workflow_index.json`, assert `workflow_from_template` resolves it.

### Step 5: Dedupe RunPod scripts behind `scripts/runpod_runner.py` (`scripts/runpod_runner.py`, three runpod entrypoints)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `scripts/runpod_runner.py` exposing:
   - `class PodGuard` (current shape — `launch()` / `terminate()` / `_start_watchdog()` / `_terminate_after()`), parameterized on `name_prefix` and `max_runtime_seconds_env`.
   - `should_skip(path, root, exclude_set)` and `upload_dir(sftp, local, remote, exclude_set)`.
   - `install_signal_handlers(loop)`.
   - `async def run_pod(remote_script: str, *, name_prefix: str, exclude: set[str], upload_mode: Literal["sftp_walk", "tarball"] = "sftp_walk", timeout: int) -> int` orchestrating launch → wait → upload → exec → terminate.
2. **Rewrite** each `runpod_*.py` to import shared helpers and define only its own `EXCLUDE_DIRS`, remote bash script string, and `main()`. Keep file paths and entrypoint names identical.
3. **Verify** `python -c "import scripts.runpod_validate"` (and the other two) succeeds before any pod call.
4. **Do not** auto-launch pods; user re-runs after merge.

### Step 6: Expand `README.md`
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** the 12-line stub. Cover: project goal, the new module layout (`vibecomfy/{workflow,ingest,registry,runtime,schema,analysis,search,commands}`), CLI subcommand list with one-line descriptions, the `sources sync` → `workflows list` → `inspect` → `convert` → `validate` → `analyze info` → `search` → `run` quick start against `workflow_corpus/`, and links to `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md` and `docs/vibeworkflow.md`.
2. **Sweep** `docs/*.md` for any reference to `vibecomfy.templates` or `vibecomfy/templates/`. Today only `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:76` mentions templates conceptually (no import-path string) — no change required, but re-grep after Step 3 to confirm.

## Phase 2: Schema-aware validation (`vibecomfy/schema/`)

### Step 7: Add a schema provider that talks to `/object_info` (`vibecomfy/schema/__init__.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/cache.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** `NodeSchema` dataclass — `class_type: str`, `pack: str | None`, `inputs: dict[str, InputSpec]` (where `InputSpec = {type, required, default, choices, min, max}`), `outputs: list[OutputSpec]` (`type, name`).
2. **Implement** `RuntimeSchemaProvider`:
   - First check the existing `vibecomfy/runtime/client.py` for an `/object_info` helper; if absent, add a thin `httpx.AsyncClient.get("/object_info")` call there (it's the natural home, alongside `queue_prompt`).
   - Reuses `vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:comfy_server` to start an embedded server on demand and cache aggressively under `out/cache/object_info.<runtime_fingerprint>.json`. Externally-running server URLs are also accepted (matching `vibecomfy run --server-url …`).
3. **Implement** `LocalSchemaProvider` — falls back to `node_index.json` (already produced by `sources sync`) when no runtime is reachable; provides `class_type`, `pack`, and a coarse input list (no per-input type info).
4. **Expose** `get_schema_provider(prefer="runtime"|"local"|"auto")` factory.

### Step 8: Use schema during UI→API normalization fallback (`vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **Today** `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py:60-63` writes positional `widget_<i>` keys when `comfy.component_model.workflow_convert` isn't importable.
2. **With schema available**, map widget positional values to declared input names from `NodeSchema.inputs` (in declared order), preserving fallback to `widget_<i>` only when the schema lookup yields nothing for that `class_type`.
3. **Skip** any input already populated by an incoming edge (don't overwrite a connection with a positional widget value).
4. **Wire** the provider in via an optional kwarg `convert_to_vibe_format(api_workflow, *, schema_provider=None, ...)`; commands that already pass schema (validate/doctor) reuse the same provider.

### Step 9: Wire schema-backed validation into `VibeWorkflow.validate()` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`, per-caller)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Reuse** the existing `vibecomfy.workflow.ValidationIssue` and `ValidationReport` types — do **not** define a parallel `ValidationIssue` in `vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`. Issue codes added: `unknown_class_type`, `missing_required_input`, `unknown_input`, `type_mismatch`.
2. **Add** `vibecomfy/schema/validate.py:validate_against_schema(workflow, provider) -> list[ValidationIssue]` (importing `ValidationIssue` from `vibecomfy.workflow`) that checks:
   - `class_type` exists in schema (`unknown_class_type`, severity `error`).
   - Required inputs are present in `node.inputs ∪ node.widgets ∪ <incoming edges>` (`missing_required_input`, error).
   - No unknown keys in `node.inputs ∪ node.widgets` (`unknown_input`, error).
   - For each edge, source's declared output type matches target's declared input type — string-equal, or one side is `*` / `ANY` (`type_mismatch`, severity `warning` initially, since custom nodes lie about types).
3. **Extend** `VibeWorkflow.validate()` signature in `vibecomfy/workflow.py:120` to `validate(self, schema_provider=None) -> ValidationReport`. When `schema_provider is None`, behavior is **identical to today** (existing tests untouched). When supplied, structural issues + schema issues are merged in one report.
4. **Per-caller wiring decisions** (explicit, so nothing slips through):
   - `commands/validate.py` — construct `get_schema_provider("auto")` and pass it. Default behavior of `vibecomfy validate <path>` now does schema checks.
   - `commands/doctor.py` — same as validate.
   - `vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:28` — **do NOT pass a schema provider.** Stays structural-only. The run path should not pay schema lookup cost on every invocation; if a user wants schema validation, they run `vibecomfy validate` first. Document this in a one-line comment at the call site.
   - `tests/test_workflow_core.py` and `tests/test_scratchpad_loader.py` — pass nothing. Existing `validate().ok` assertions stay valid because the structural-only branch is unchanged.

### Step 10: Tests for schema validation (`tests/test_schema.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Cover** with a fake `SchemaProvider` (no real runtime):
   - Unknown `class_type` emits one `ValidationIssue(code="unknown_class_type", severity="error")`.
   - Required input missing emits one issue with the input name in the message.
   - Unknown input emits one issue.
   - Type-mismatched edge emits one issue with `severity="warning"`.
   - Confirm issues are `vibecomfy.workflow.ValidationIssue` (`isinstance` check) — guards against the parallel-type regression.
   - When `schema_provider=None`, `validate()` return matches today's structural-only behavior on the fixture used by `tests/test_workflow_core.py`.
2. **Run** `pytest -q tests/test_schema.py` then full suite.

## Phase 3: Graph analysis module (`vibecomfy/analysis/`)

### Step 11: Build the analysis primitives on `VibeWorkflow` (`vibecomfy/analysis/graph.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** as pure functions taking `VibeWorkflow`, no I/O:
   - `analyze(workflow) -> dict` — node count, edge count, fan-in/fan-out histogram, output sinks, terminal inputs, detected media type.
   - `trace(workflow, node_id) -> list[VibeNode]` — full upstream chain.
   - `upstream(workflow, node_id, depth=None) -> set[str]`.
   - `downstream(workflow, node_id, depth=None) -> set[str]`.
   - `path(workflow, src, dst) -> list[list[str]]` — all simple paths, capped at depth 32.
   - `subgraph(workflow, node_ids) -> VibeWorkflow` — new VibeWorkflow with only given nodes and edges between them; never mutates source.
   - `values(workflow, node_id=None) -> dict` — literal `inputs ∪ widgets`, skipping linked inputs.
   - `diff(workflow_a, workflow_b) -> dict` — added/removed/changed nodes by id+class_type, edge add/remove sets, input value changes.
   - `unconnected(workflow, schema_provider=None) -> list[dict]` — nodes whose required inputs are not satisfied by edges or values. With schema, uses `NodeSchema.inputs[*].required`; without, reports nodes with no incoming edges and no widget defaults for non-source nodes.
2. **Reuse** existing `VibeWorkflow`, `VibeNode`, `VibeEdge`, `VibeOutput` types — no parallel shapes.

### Step 12: Expose analysis as CLI subcommands (`vibecomfy/commands/analyze.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **Add** parent subcommand `vibecomfy analyze` with sub-subcommands `info`, `trace`, `upstream`, `downstream`, `path`, `subgraph`, `values`, `diff`, `unconnected` (settled: bundled, not nine top-level verbs).
2. **Each** sub-subcommand accepts a workflow positional resolved by the **same** `_resolve_workflow_path` already used by `inspect`/`convert` (which correctly walks both indexes — see Step 4 fix that aligns library behavior with this resolver). Verb-specific args: `--node-id`, `--src`, `--dst`, `--max-depth`, `--format json|tsv|text`.
3. **Default** human-readable output; `--format json` emits machine-readable.
4. **Wire** into the parser builder via `commands/__init__.py`.

### Step 13: Tests for analysis (`tests/test_analysis.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Build** a small synthetic `VibeWorkflow` (the fixture in `tests/test_workflow_core.py:test_api_workflow_converts_to_vibe_workflow` is a good seed) and assert each function returns the expected sets/lists. Cover `diff` with a small mutation. Cover `subgraph` non-mutation by re-asserting source workflow equality.
2. **CLI smoke**: `subprocess.run([..., "vibecomfy", "analyze", "info", "<fixture>.json"])` returns 0 with non-empty stdout.

## Phase 4: Node search (`vibecomfy/search/`)

### Step 14: Search bootstrap — guarantee indexes exist before search runs (`vibecomfy/search/bootstrap.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/search.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Today** `node_index.json` is written by `sources sync`, but `vibecomfy/nodes/index.py:index_custom_node_examples` is invoked inline and its results are folded into `external_workflow_index.json`. There is no guarantee either index exists on a fresh checkout.
2. **Add** `vibecomfy/search/bootstrap.py:ensure_indexes(*, auto_sync: bool = False) -> None`:
   - Checks for `node_index.json`, `template_index.json`, `external_workflow_index.json`.
   - If `auto_sync=False` and any are missing → raise a `SearchBootstrapError("indexes missing; run `vibecomfy sources sync` first")`.
   - If `auto_sync=True` → invoke the same code path `vibecomfy sources sync` runs (factor a shared helper out of `commands/sources.py:_cmd_sources_sync` if needed; keep the CLI handler thin).
3. **Default** for `vibecomfy search`: call `ensure_indexes(auto_sync=False)` and surface the bootstrap error with exit code 1 + clear message. Add `--auto-sync` flag for convenience.

### Step 15: Build the search index and weighted scorer (`vibecomfy/search/index.py`, `vibecomfy/search/aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/search/scorer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** `SearchEntry` — `class_type`, `pack`, `description`, `tags: list[str]`, `tasks: list[str]`, `source: Literal["object_info","node_index","custom_node_examples","curated"]`, `path: str | None`.
2. **Build the corpus** by merging (after `ensure_indexes`):
   - Runtime schema provider (Step 7) — full input/output type info per `class_type`.
   - `node_index.json` — runtime-known nodes.
   - Custom-node example workflows by re-using `vibecomfy/nodes/index.py:index_custom_node_examples` — each example contributes the union of `class_type`s it uses (lower weight).
   - Curated metadata from `workflow_corpus/manifests/coverage.json` — gives `task` + `model_family` + `media`.
3. **Add** `vibecomfy/search/aliases.py` with the task-alias map: `i2v`, `t2v`, `t2i`, `controlnet`, `wan`, `ltx`, `audio_reactive`, `flux`, `sdxl`, `qwen`, `hunyuan`, `inpaint`, `outpaint` — each expanding to substring/regex hits against `class_type` / `tags` / `description` / `tasks`.
4. **Implement** weighted scorer: exact `class_type` match (5) > pack match (3) > tag match (2) > description token (1); alias hits add +2 each (stack). Return top-N descending.

### Step 16: Add CLI commands `vibecomfy search` and `vibecomfy nodes spec` (`vibecomfy/commands/search.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/nodes.py`)
**Scope:** Small-Medium
1. **`vibecomfy search <query>`** — free text; supports `--task <alias>`, `--limit`, `--auto-sync`. Calls `ensure_indexes` (Step 14), prints id / class_type / pack / score / source.
2. **Extend** `vibecomfy nodes` (today: only `list`) with `nodes spec <class_type>` — print the full `NodeSchema` from the schema provider.
3. **Wire** into the parser builder.

### Step 17: Tests for search (`tests/test_search.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Fixture** with a small in-memory `SearchEntry` list; assert task aliases produce expected hits, exact `class_type` wins over partial, pack-match ranks above description-only.
2. **Bootstrap test**: with no index files in `tmp_path` and CWD swapped, assert `ensure_indexes(auto_sync=False)` raises and the CLI surfaces a clear error pointing at `vibecomfy sources sync`.
3. **Smoke**: `vibecomfy search wan --task i2v` against the local `workflow_corpus/` returns at least one entry (developer-box check; CI-acceptable as long as `sources sync` is run first in the test fixture).

## Phase 5: Validation rollup

### Step 18: Run the existing test suite + manual CLI smokes
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `python -m pytest -q tests` — all existing tests still pass after the rename (Step 3) and the optional `schema_provider` param (Step 9). The new tests in Steps 10, 13, 17 also pass.
2. **CLI quick-start** against the local corpus:
   ```bash
   python -m vibecomfy.cli sources sync
   python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows list --limit 5
   python -m vibecomfy.cli inspect z_image
   python -m vibecomfy.cli analyze info workflow_corpus/official/image/z_image.json
   python -m vibecomfy.cli search wan --task i2v
   python -m vibecomfy.cli validate examples/smoke_empty_image_red.json
   ```
3. **Skip** GPU-bound runpod scripts in CI; just confirm `python -c "import scripts.runpod_validate"` (and the other two) succeeds.

### Step 19: Confirm RunPod entrypoints still launch (info-only)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Document** in PR description that `runpod_validate.py` / `runpod_model_matrix.py` / `runpod_corpus_matrix.py` were not actually run (cost). Step 3 updated the embedded heredocs and Step 5 dedup'd the launcher; reviewer/user re-runs after merge. Info-priority criterion only.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 first — structural cleanup unblocks the new module homes; doing it after the ports forces two rounds of import churn.
2. Within Phase 1: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6. The rename in Step 3 must happen before Step 4's library fix (which lives in the new file location).
3. Phase 2 (schema) before Phase 3 and Phase 4 — both depend on the schema provider.
4. Phase 3 (analysis) and Phase 4 (search) serialize 3 → 4 to keep diffs reviewable. Search depends on Step 4's resolver fix and on the schema provider from Phase 2.
5. Phase 5 last — validates the whole.

## Validation Order
1. After each Phase 1 step, run `pytest -q tests` + targeted CLI smokes (sources sync against the new defaults; rename verified by `Grep -r "vibecomfy\.templates"` returning zero hits).
2. After Steps 9, 11, 14–16, run the new module's targeted tests first, then full `pytest`.
3. After Phase 5, run the manual CLI quick-start from Step 18.
4. RunPod scripts: import-only check; do not auto-run pods.

  {
    "id": "FLAG-001",
    "concern": "Schema issue type duplication: the plan introduces vibecomfy/schema/validate.py:validate_against_schema(...) -> list[ValidationIssue] without saying to reuse the existing vibecomfy.workflow.ValidationIssue, risking incompatible issue objects in callers that already expect the workflow module's ValidationReport/ValidationIssue shape.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-002",
    "concern": "Search bootstrap gap: vibecomfy search depends on object_info, node_index.json, examples, and curated metadata, but the plan does not guarantee indexes exist or are refreshed on a fresh checkout before search runs.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-003",
    "concern": "Template-id resolution gap: current workflow_from_template ignores external_workflow_index.json whenever template_index.json exists, and the plan preserves that behavior while adding more path-or-template-id analysis commands over both official and external corpus entries.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "verifiability-0",
    "concern": "Criterion 14: requires human verification (subjective_judgment).",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "verifiability-1",
    "concern": "Criterion 17: requires human verification (verify_physical_device).",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "issue_hints",
    "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: The plan asks seven open questions even though the user request already fixes several constraints for this review/execution context. In particular, preserving all existing CLI commands, deferring port-after-redesign work, and keeping RunPod entrypoints are hard requirements, not decisions to leave unresolved during implementation; this could let an executor pause or choose a different surface than the brief intended.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "correctness",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Checked VibeWorkflow.validate() and its callers: the current contract already returns ValidationReport with ValidationIssue objects, not strings. The plan's schema validation can fit that model if it returns the same issue class from vibecomfy.workflow or a deliberately compatible class; otherwise, adding a second ValidationIssue definition in vibecomfy/schema/validate.py would make downstream issue handling inconsistent.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Searched for vibecomfy.templates imports and found references in the package, tests, and RunPod embedded remote Python snippets. The plan names package/test/runpod updates, so the internal caller set is covered, but a clean break without any docs update would leave existing documentation/examples stale if they mention or later add the old import path.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-1",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Checked vibecomfy/nodes/index.py and vibecomfy/templates/index.py: custom-node example indexing and workflow-source indexing are separate modules today. The search plan says custom-node examples contribute via existing index_custom_node_examples, but does not schedule any refresh path that ensures node_index.json and custom-node examples are available before vibecomfy search; without that glue, search may rank only curated/local schema data on a fresh checkout.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-2",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Checked vibecomfy/templates/library.py and CLI resolver behavior: workflow_from_template currently reads template_index.json first, then falls back to external_workflow_index.json only if template_index.json does not exist. This means external workflow ids are not resolvable whenever the official index exists; the plan's module move preserves this existing bug but does not fix it, which matters because the new analysis/search CLI wants to accept path-or-template ids across the corpus.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Grep found VibeWorkflow.validate() is called from vibecomfy/cli.py, vibecomfy/runtime/run.py, and tests/test_workflow_core.py plus tests/test_scratchpad_loader.py. These callers currently pass no arguments and expect a ValidationReport with .ok and .issues; the plan preserves schema_provider=None, but implementation should update validate/doctor only, because runtime/run should probably remain structural-only unless the run path intentionally pays the schema lookup cost.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  }
]

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
